where I try to model, you know, I try to have a mental model

03:31:04 --> 03:31:06
of like the two different sides of a serious argument.

03:31:05 --> 03:31:08
And so I've tried to like internalize that argument

03:31:07 --> 03:31:10
as much as I can, which is difficult

03:31:09 --> 03:31:12
'cause like we're investing it behind LLMs

03:31:11 --> 03:31:12
as aggressively as we can.

03:31:11 --> 03:31:16
And so if he's right, like that could be a big problem.

03:31:15 --> 03:31:18
But like, we should also know that.

03:31:17 --> 03:31:20
And then I sort of use his ideas

03:31:19 --> 03:31:23
to challenge all the bullish people, you know,

03:31:22 --> 03:31:25
to really kind of test their level of knowledge.

03:31:24 --> 03:31:27
So I like to kind of grill people.

03:31:26 --> 03:31:32
Like, you know, I was got my CS degree 35 years ago,

03:31:32 --> 03:31:34
so I'm not like deep in the technology,

03:31:33 --> 03:31:37
but like to the extent I can understand Yann's points,

03:31:36 --> 03:31:39
I can use them to, you know, to really surface a lot

03:31:38 --> 03:31:42
of the questions for the people who are more bullish.

03:31:41 --> 03:31:44
And that's been, I think, very, very productive.

03:31:44 --> 03:31:46
Yeah, just, and it's very striking

03:31:45 --> 03:31:48
that you have somebody who is like

03:31:47 --> 03:31:49
that central in the space,

03:31:48 --> 03:31:52
who is actually like a full-on skeptic.

03:31:52 --> 03:31:55
And again, this could go different ways.

03:31:54 --> 03:31:57
He could end up being very wrong.

03:31:56 --> 03:31:58
He could end up being totally right,

03:31:57 --> 03:32:01
or it could be that he will provoke the evolution

03:32:00 --> 03:32:04
of these systems to be much better than they would've been.

03:32:03 --> 03:32:05
- Yeah, he could be both right and wrong.

03:32:04 --> 03:32:07
First of all, I do agree with that.

03:32:06 --> 03:32:10
He's one of the most legit and rigorous

03:32:09 --> 03:32:14
and deep critics of the LLM path to AGI, you know,

03:32:13 --> 03:32:17
his basic notions that there needs AI needs

03:32:16 --> 03:32:21
to have some physical understanding of the physical world,

03:32:20 --> 03:32:23
and that's very difficult to achieve with LLMs.

03:32:24 --> 03:32:26
And that is a really good way

03:32:25 --> 03:32:29
to challenge the limitations of LLMs and so on.

03:32:28 --> 03:32:32
He's also been a vocal

03:32:31 --> 03:32:33
and a huge proponent

03:32:32 --> 03:32:34
of open source. - Yes.

03:32:33 --> 03:32:35
- Which is a whole nother- - Yes.

03:32:34 --> 03:32:36
- Which you have been as well.

03:32:35 --> 03:32:38
- [Marc] Which is very useful. Yeah.

03:32:37 --> 03:32:39
- And that's been just fascinating to watch.

03:32:38 --> 03:32:40
- [Marc] And anti-doomer.

03:32:39 --> 03:32:42
- Anti-doomer? - Yeah.

03:32:41 --> 03:32:44
- Yeah, he's- - He's very anti-doomer.

03:32:43 --> 03:32:45
- He embodies...

03:32:44 --> 03:32:47
He also has many wolves inside.

03:32:46 --> 03:32:48
- [Marc] Yes, he does. Yes, does.

03:32:48 --> 03:32:51
- So it's been really, really fun to watch.

03:32:50 --> 03:32:52
- The other two.

03:32:51 --> 03:32:53
Okay, here's my other wolf coming out.

03:32:52 --> 03:32:54
- [Lex] Yeah.

03:32:53 --> 03:32:55
- The other two of the three godfathers of AI

03:32:54 --> 03:32:58
are like radicals,

03:32:57 --> 03:33:02
like full-on left, you know, far left, you know, like,

03:33:01 --> 03:33:05
I would say like either Marxists or borderline Marxists.

03:33:04 --> 03:33:06
And they're like,

03:33:05 --> 03:33:08
I think, quite extreme in their social political views.

03:33:07 --> 03:33:10
And I think that feeds into their doomerism,

03:33:09 --> 03:33:11
and I think, you know,

03:33:10 --> 03:33:14
they are lobbying for like draconian government,

03:33:13 --> 03:33:16
I think what would be ruinously destructive government

03:33:15 --> 03:33:18
legislation and regulation.

03:33:17 --> 03:33:19
And so it's actually super helpful,

03:33:18 --> 03:33:20
super, super helpful

03:33:19 --> 03:33:23
to have Yann as a counterpoint to those two.

03:33:22 --> 03:33:26
- Another fun question, our mutual friend Andrew Huberman.

03:33:25 --> 03:33:27
- [Marc] Yes.

03:33:26 --> 03:33:29
- First maybe, what do you love most about Andrew?

03:33:28 --> 03:33:32
And second, what score on a scale of 1 to 10

03:33:31 --> 03:33:35
do you think he would give you on your approach to health?

03:33:34 --> 03:33:37
- Oh, three.

03:33:36 --> 03:33:38
- Physical three.

03:33:37 --> 03:33:39
You think you'd score that high, huh?

03:33:38 --> 03:33:40
Okay. - (laughs) Exactly.

03:33:40 --> 03:33:43
- That's good. (laughs) - Exactly.

03:33:42 --> 03:33:46
Well, so he convinced me to stop drinking alcohol,

03:33:46 --> 03:33:48
which was a big- - Successfully?

03:33:47 --> 03:33:49
- Well, it was like other than my family,

03:33:48 --> 03:33:50
it was my favorite thing

03:33:49 --> 03:33:51
in the world. - Yeah.

03:33:50 --> 03:33:53
- And so it was a major, major reduction.

03:33:52 --> 03:33:54
Like having like a glass of scotch

03:33:53 --> 03:33:56
at night, it was like a major, like,

03:33:55 --> 03:33:57
it was like the thing I would do to relax.

03:33:56 --> 03:33:59
And so he has profoundly negatively impacted

03:33:58 --> 03:34:00
my emotional health.

03:33:59 --> 03:34:02
(Lex laughing)

03:34:01 --> 03:34:03
I blame him- - Yeah.

03:34:02 --> 03:34:04
- For making me much less happy as a person.

03:34:03 --> 03:34:06
But much, much, much healthier.

03:34:05 --> 03:34:08
- Yeah. - Physically healthier.

03:34:07 --> 03:34:10
So that I credit him with that.

03:34:09 --> 03:34:11
I'm glad I did that.

03:34:10 --> 03:34:12
But then his sleep stuff like,

03:34:11 --> 03:34:15
yeah, I'm not doing any of that.

03:34:14 --> 03:34:15
- [Lex] Yeah.

03:34:14 --> 03:34:17
- I have no interest in his sleep shit. Like, no.

03:34:17 --> 03:34:20
This whole light, natural light,

03:34:19 --> 03:34:20
no, we're not doing it.

03:34:19 --> 03:34:22
- You're too hardcore for this?

03:34:23 --> 03:34:25
- I don't see any natural light in here.

03:34:24 --> 03:34:27
- [Lex] It's all covered.

03:34:26 --> 03:34:28
It's all horrible.

03:34:27 --> 03:34:29
- And I'm very happy.

03:34:28 --> 03:34:31
I would be very happy living and working here

03:34:30 --> 03:34:33
'cause I'm totally happy without natural light.

03:34:32 --> 03:34:35
- In darkness. - Yes.

03:34:34 --> 03:34:36
- It must be a metaphor for something.

03:34:35 --> 03:34:37
- Yes, it's a test.

03:34:36 --> 03:34:38
Look, it's a test of manhood

03:34:37 --> 03:34:40
as to whether you can have a blue screen in your face

03:34:39 --> 03:34:41
for three hours and then go right to sleep.

03:34:40 --> 03:34:42
Like, I don't understand

03:34:41 --> 03:34:44
why you should wanna take shortcuts.

03:34:45 --> 03:34:49
- I now understand what they mean by toxic masculinity.

03:34:48 --> 03:34:51
All right.

03:34:50 --> 03:34:53
(both laughing)

03:34:52 --> 03:34:55
So let's see.

03:34:54 --> 03:35:00
You're exceptionally successful by most measures,

03:34:59 --> 03:35:02
but what to you is the definition of success?

03:35:02 --> 03:35:07
- I would probably say it is a combination of two things,

03:35:06 --> 03:35:11
I think it is contribution.

03:35:11 --> 03:35:14
So, you know, have you done something

03:35:13 --> 03:35:15
that mattered ultimately,

03:35:17 --> 03:35:20
you know, and specifically it mattered to people?

03:35:19 --> 03:35:21
And then the other thing is,

03:35:20 --> 03:35:23
I think happiness is either overrated

03:35:22 --> 03:35:24
or almost a complete myth.

03:35:25 --> 03:35:27
And in fact, interesting,

03:35:26 --> 03:35:28
Thomas Jefferson did not mean happiness the way

03:35:27 --> 03:35:29
that we understand it.

03:35:28 --> 03:35:31
When he said, "Pursuit of happiness"

03:35:30 --> 03:35:32
in the "Declaration of Independence,"

03:35:31 --> 03:35:34
he meant it more of the Greek meaning,

03:35:34 --> 03:35:39
which is closer to satisfaction or fulfillment.

03:35:39 --> 03:35:42
And so I think about happiness

03:35:41 --> 03:35:46
as the first ice cream cone makes you super happy.

03:35:45 --> 03:35:49
The first mile of the walk in the park

03:35:48 --> 03:35:51
during sunset makes you super happy.

03:35:50 --> 03:35:53
The first kiss makes you super happy.

03:35:52 --> 03:35:56
The thousandth ice cream cone, not so much.

03:35:56 --> 03:36:00
The thousandth mile of the walk through the park.

03:36:00 --> 03:36:02
The thousandth kiss can still be good,

03:36:01 --> 03:36:04
but maybe just not right in a row.

03:36:04 --> 03:36:08
Right, and so happiness is this very fleeting concept,

03:36:07 --> 03:36:10
and the people who anchor on happiness seem

03:36:09 --> 03:36:12
to go off the rails pretty often.

03:36:11 --> 03:36:15
So did the deep sense of having been,

03:36:15 --> 03:36:18
I dunno how to put it useful.

03:36:20 --> 03:36:24
- So that's a good place to arrive at in life.

03:36:23 --> 03:36:25
- Yeah, I think so.

03:36:24 --> 03:36:27
Yeah, I mean, like, can you sit, yeah.

03:36:26 --> 03:36:29
You know, who was it who said,

03:36:28 --> 03:36:30
the source of all the ills in the world

03:36:29 --> 03:36:32
is man's inability to sit in a room

03:36:31 --> 03:36:33
by himself doing nothing.

03:36:34 --> 03:36:36
But like, if you're sitting in a room

03:36:35 --> 03:36:37
by yourself and you're like, all right.

03:36:36 --> 03:36:40
Or you know, four in the morning, it's like, all right,

03:36:39 --> 03:36:43
have I lived up to my expectation of myself?

03:36:42 --> 03:36:45
Like if you have, you know, the people I know who feel

03:36:44 --> 03:36:46
that way are pretty centered

03:36:45 --> 03:36:51
and, you know, generally seem very, I dunno how to put it,

03:36:50 --> 03:36:55
pleased with, you know, proud, calm, at peace.

03:36:55 --> 03:36:59
The people who are, you know, sensation seekers,

03:37:00 --> 03:37:03
you know, some of the sensations by the way, some sense,

03:37:02 --> 03:37:04
you know, there's certain entrepreneurs, for example,

03:37:03 --> 03:37:06
who are like into every form of extreme sport

03:37:05 --> 03:37:09
and they get, you know, huge satisfaction out of that.

03:37:08 --> 03:37:10
Or, you know, there's sensation seeking

03:37:09 --> 03:37:12
in sort of useful and productive ways.

03:37:11 --> 03:37:13
You know, Larry Ellison was always like that.

03:37:12 --> 03:37:15
Zuckerberg is like that.

03:37:15 --> 03:37:17
And then, you know, there's a lot of entrepreneurs

03:37:16 --> 03:37:19
who end up, you know, drugs, (laughs) you know,

03:37:20 --> 03:37:23
like you know, sexual escapades

03:37:23 --> 03:37:26
that seem like they'll be fun at first and then backfire.

03:37:25 --> 03:37:28
- Yeah, but at the end of the day,

03:37:27 --> 03:37:29
if you're able to be at peace

03:37:29 --> 03:37:32
by yourself in a room at 4:00 AM-

03:37:31 --> 03:37:34
[Marc] Yeah. - And I would even say happy,

03:37:33 --> 03:37:37
but I know, I understand Thomas Jefferson

03:37:36 --> 03:37:41
didn't mean it the way, maybe I mean it,

03:37:40 --> 03:37:43
but I can be happy by myself at 4:00 AM-

03:37:42 --> 03:37:44
Yeah. - With a blue screen.

03:37:43 --> 03:37:45
- That's good. Exactly.

03:37:44 --> 03:37:47
- Staring at cursor. - Exactly.

03:37:49 --> 03:37:52
- As a small tangent,

03:37:51 --> 03:37:54
a quick shout out to an amazing interview you did

03:37:53 --> 03:37:56
with Bari Weiss and just to her in general,

03:37:55 --> 03:37:59
Bari Weiss of the Free Press.

03:37:58 --> 03:38:01
She has a podcast called, "Honestly, with Bari Weiss."

03:38:00 --> 03:38:02
She's great.

03:38:01 --> 03:38:03
People should go listen.

03:38:02 --> 03:38:07
You were asked if you believe in God.

03:38:08 --> 03:38:10
One of the joys...

03:38:09 --> 03:38:11
See, we talked about happiness.

03:38:10 --> 03:38:12
One of the things that makes me happy

03:38:11 --> 03:38:14
is making you uncomfortable.

03:38:13 --> 03:38:15
- [Marc] Thank you.

03:38:14 --> 03:38:17
- So this question is designed for many

03:38:16 --> 03:38:19
of the questions today were designed for that.

03:38:18 --> 03:38:21
You were asked if you believe in God,

03:38:20 --> 03:38:24
and you said after a pause that you're not sure.

03:38:23 --> 03:38:28
So it felt like the pause, the uncertainty,

03:38:27 --> 03:38:33
there was some kind of ongoing search for wisdom

03:38:32 --> 03:38:34
and meaning.

03:38:34 --> 03:38:38
Are you, in fact, searching for wisdom and meaning?

03:38:37 --> 03:38:39
- I guess I'd put it this way.

03:38:38 --> 03:38:44
There's a lot to just understand about people

03:38:43 --> 03:38:47
that I feel like I'm only starting to understand.

03:38:49 --> 03:38:53
And that's certainly a simpler concept than God. (laughs)

03:38:52 --> 03:38:56
So that's what I've spent a lot of the last, you know,

03:38:55 --> 03:38:57
15 years trying to figure out.

03:38:56 --> 03:38:59
I feel like I spent my first like whatever,

03:38:58 --> 03:39:01
30 years figuring out machines,

03:39:00 --> 03:39:03
and then now I'm spending 30 years figuring out people,

03:39:02 --> 03:39:06
which turns out to be quite a bit more complicated.

03:39:05 --> 03:39:07
And then, I don't know, maybe God's the last 30 years

03:39:06 --> 03:39:08
or something.

03:39:10 --> 03:39:12
And then, you know, look, I mean just, you know,

03:39:11 --> 03:39:13
like Elon, it's just like, okay,

03:39:12 --> 03:39:16
the known universe is like very, you know, complicated

03:39:15 --> 03:39:18
and, you know, mystifying.

03:39:17 --> 03:39:20
I mean, every time I, you know, pull up an astronomy,

03:39:19 --> 03:39:21
my kid super in astronomy,

03:39:20 --> 03:39:22
and it's like, you know,

03:39:21 --> 03:39:24
"Daddy, how many galaxies are there in the universe?"

03:39:23 --> 03:39:25
And you know,

03:39:24 --> 03:39:27
how many galaxies are there in the universe?

03:39:26 --> 03:39:30
- 100 billion? - Okay. Like how?

03:39:29 --> 03:39:31
(Lex laughing)

03:39:30 --> 03:39:32
- Yeah.

03:39:31 --> 03:39:33
- Like- - Yeah.

03:39:32 --> 03:39:35
- Like how that freaking possible?

03:39:34 --> 03:39:39
Like it's such a staggering concept that I-

03:39:38 --> 03:39:42
I actually wanted to show you a tweet

03:39:41 --> 03:39:44
hat blew my mind from Elon from a while back.

03:39:44 --> 03:39:46
Elon, said,

03:39:45 --> 03:39:49
"As a friend called it, this is the ultimate skill tree.

03:39:48 --> 03:39:52
This is a wall of galaxies,

03:39:51 --> 03:39:55
a billion light years across." - Yeah.

03:39:54 --> 03:39:57
- So these are all galaxies. - Yeah.

03:39:57 --> 03:40:00
Like how was it that big?

03:39:59 --> 03:40:02
Like how the hell, and like, you know,

03:40:01 --> 03:40:04
I can read the textbook and the this and the that

03:40:03 --> 03:40:05
and the whatever, 8 billion years and the Big Bang

03:40:04 --> 03:40:06
and the whole thing.

03:40:05 --> 03:40:08
And then it's just like, all right, wow.

03:40:07 --> 03:40:09
And then it's like, all rights, the Big Bang.

03:40:08 --> 03:40:14
All right, like what was before the Big Bang? (laughs)

03:40:13 --> 03:40:18
- Do you think we humans will ever colonize like a galaxy

03:40:17 --> 03:40:20
and maybe even go beyond?

03:40:19 --> 03:40:22
- Sure. Yeah, I mean, in the fullness of time.

03:40:21 --> 03:40:23
Yeah.

03:40:22 --> 03:40:24
- So you have that kind of optimism.

03:40:23 --> 03:40:25
You have that kind of hope that extends

03:40:24 --> 03:40:27
across a thousand of- - In the fullness of time.

03:40:26 --> 03:40:28
I mean, yeah, you know,

03:40:27 --> 03:40:29
all the problem, all the challenges with it that I do,

03:40:28 --> 03:40:31
but like, yeah, why not?

03:40:31 --> 03:40:33
I mean, again, in the fullness of time,

03:40:32 --> 03:40:34
it'll take a long time.

03:40:33 --> 03:40:35
- You don't think we'll destroy ourselves?

03:40:34 --> 03:40:37
- No, I doubt it. I doubt it.

03:40:36 --> 03:40:39
And you know, fortunately we have Elon giving us,

03:40:38 --> 03:40:40
(Lex laughing)

03:40:39 --> 03:40:42
giving us the backup plan.

03:40:41 --> 03:40:43
So I don't know, like, I grew up, you know,

03:40:42 --> 03:40:45
real Midwest, sort of just like conventionally

03:40:44 --> 03:40:47
kind of Protestant Christian.

03:40:46 --> 03:40:48
It never made that much sense to me.

03:40:47 --> 03:40:50
Got trained as an engineer and a scientist.

03:40:49 --> 03:40:52
I'm like, "Oh, that definitely doesn't make sense."

03:40:51 --> 03:40:53
I'm like, "I know I'll spend my life

03:40:52 --> 03:40:54
as an empirical, you know, rationalist

03:40:53 --> 03:40:55
and I'll figure everything out."

03:40:54 --> 03:40:57
And then, you know, and then again,

03:40:56 --> 03:41:00
you walk up against these things, you know,

03:40:59 --> 03:41:01
you bump up against these things

03:41:00 --> 03:41:02
and you're just like, "All right,"

03:41:01 --> 03:41:05
I like, "Okay, I guess there's a scientific explanation

03:41:04 --> 03:41:07
for this, but like, wow."

03:41:07 --> 03:41:09
And then there's like,

03:41:08 --> 03:41:11
"All right, where did that come from?"

03:41:10 --> 03:41:12
Right, and then how far back can you go

03:41:11 --> 03:41:14
n the causality chain?

03:41:14 --> 03:41:17
Yeah. And then, yeah, I mean, and even just, you know,

03:41:16 --> 03:41:19
experiences that we all have on earth,

03:41:18 --> 03:41:20
it's hard to rationally explain it all.

03:41:19 --> 03:41:22
And then, you know, so yeah,

03:41:21 --> 03:41:25
I guess I just say I'm kind of radically open-minded,

03:41:24 --> 03:41:28
at peace with the fact that I'll probably never know.

03:41:27 --> 03:41:29
The other thing though, that's happened,

03:41:28 --> 03:41:31
and maybe the more practical answer to the question

03:41:30 --> 03:41:33
is I think I have a much better understanding now

03:41:32 --> 03:41:36
of the role that religion plays in society

03:41:35 --> 03:41:38
that I didn't have when I was younger.

03:41:37 --> 03:41:41
And my partner, Ben has a great...

03:41:40 --> 03:41:42
I think he quotes his father on this.

03:41:41 --> 03:41:44
He's like, "If a man does not have a real religion,

03:41:43 --> 03:41:45
he makes up a fake one,

03:41:46 --> 03:41:49
and the fake ones go very, very badly."

03:41:48 --> 03:41:53
And so there's this class, it's actually really funny.

03:41:53 --> 03:41:55
There's this class of intellectual

03:41:54 --> 03:41:56
that has what appears

03:41:55 --> 03:41:58
to be a very patronizing point of view, which is,

03:41:57 --> 03:42:00
"Yes, I'm an atheist, but it's very important

03:41:59 --> 03:42:02
hat the people believe in something."

03:42:01 --> 03:42:04
Right?

03:42:03 --> 03:42:05
And Marx had like the negative view on that,

03:42:04 --> 03:42:07
which is religions the opiate of the masses.

03:42:06 --> 03:42:09
But there's a lot of like right-wing intellectuals

03:42:08 --> 03:42:09
who are themselves, I think,

03:42:08 --> 03:42:11
pretty atheist or agnostic,

03:42:10 --> 03:42:12
that are like, it's deeply important

03:42:11 --> 03:42:15
hat the people be Christian or something like that.

03:42:14 --> 03:42:16
And on the one hand it's like,

03:42:15 --> 03:42:17
wow, that's arrogant and presumptive.

03:42:16 --> 03:42:19
But on the other hand, you know,

03:42:18 --> 03:42:21
maybe it's right because, you know,

03:42:20 --> 03:42:23
what have we learned in the last 100 years

03:42:22 --> 03:42:25
is in the absence of a real religion,

03:42:24 --> 03:42:27
people will make up fake ones.

03:42:27 --> 03:42:30
There's this writer, there's this political philosopher

03:42:29 --> 03:42:32
who's super interesting on this named Eric Voegelin.

03:42:31 --> 03:42:36
And he wrote in the sort of mid-part of the century,

03:42:35 --> 03:42:38
mid-late-part of the 20th century,

03:42:37 --> 03:42:39
he was like born in, I think,

03:42:38 --> 03:42:40
like 1900, and like died in like '85.

03:42:39 --> 03:42:44
So he saw the complete run of communism and Nazism

03:42:44 --> 03:42:46
and himself, you know,

03:42:45 --> 03:42:49
I think he fled Europe and you know, the whole thing.

03:42:48 --> 03:42:53
And you know, his sort of big conclusion was basically

03:42:52 --> 03:42:55
that both communism and Nazism, fascism,

03:42:54 --> 03:42:57
were basically religions,

03:42:56 --> 03:43:00
but like in the deep way of religions.

03:42:59 --> 03:43:02
Like, you know, we call 'em political religions,

03:43:01 --> 03:43:03
but they were like actual religions.

03:43:02 --> 03:43:06
And you know, they were what Nietzsche forecasted

03:43:05 --> 03:43:08
when he said, you know, "God is dead.

03:43:07 --> 03:43:10
We've killed him, and we won't wash the blood off our hands

03:43:09 --> 03:43:12
for 1,000 years," right?

03:43:11 --> 03:43:14
Is we will come up with new religions

03:43:13 --> 03:43:16
that will just cause just mass murder and death.

03:43:16 --> 03:43:18
And like, you read his stuff now

03:43:17 --> 03:43:21
and you're like, "Yep, (laughs) that happened."

03:43:20 --> 03:43:23
Right? And then, of course, as fully, you know,

03:43:22 --> 03:43:24
elite moderates, of course,

03:43:23 --> 03:43:26
we couldn't possibly be doing that for ourselves right now,

03:43:25 --> 03:43:28
but of course, we are.

03:43:27 --> 03:43:30
And you know, I would argue that Eric Voegelin, for sure,

03:43:29 --> 03:43:32
would argue that the last 10 years, you know,

03:43:31 --> 03:43:34
we have been in a religious frenzy, you know,

03:43:33 --> 03:43:37
that woke has been a full scale religious frenzy

03:43:36 --> 03:43:40
and has had all of the characteristics of a religion,

03:43:39 --> 03:43:41
including everything from patron saints

03:43:40 --> 03:43:44
to holy texts, to, you know, sin.

03:43:48 --> 03:43:50
Wokeness has said, every,

03:43:49 --> 03:43:51
I think, has said every single aspect

03:43:50 --> 03:43:55
of an actual religion other than redemption, right?

03:43:54 --> 03:43:57
Which is maybe like the most dangerous religion

03:43:56 --> 03:43:58
you could ever come up with,

03:43:57 --> 03:44:01
is the one where there's no forgiveness, right?

03:44:00 --> 03:44:02
And so I think if Voegelin were alive,

03:44:01 --> 03:44:03
I think he would've zeroed right in on that,

03:44:02 --> 03:44:05
would've said that.

03:44:04 --> 03:44:07
And, you know, we just like sailed right off.

03:44:06 --> 03:44:09
I mentioned earlier like we somehow rediscovered

03:44:08 --> 03:44:10
the religions of the Indo-Europeans.

03:44:09 --> 03:44:14
We're all into identity politics and environmentalism.

03:44:13 --> 03:44:16
Like, I don't think that's an accident.

03:44:15 --> 03:44:20
So anyway, like there is something very deep going on

03:44:19 --> 03:44:23
in the human psyche, on religion,

03:44:22 --> 03:44:28
that is not dismissible and needs to be taken seriously.

03:44:29 --> 03:44:33
Even if one struggles with the specifics of it.

03:44:33 --> 03:44:35
- I think I speak for a lot of people

03:44:34 --> 03:44:37
that it's been a real joy

03:44:36 --> 03:44:42
and for me, an honor to get to watch you seek to understand

03:44:41 --> 03:44:44
the human psyche as you described.

03:44:43 --> 03:44:46
You're in that 30-year part of your life,

03:44:47 --> 03:44:50
and it's been an honor to talk with you today.

03:44:49 --> 03:44:51
Thank you, Marc.

03:44:50 --> 03:44:52
- Thank you, Lex.

03:44:51 --> 03:44:53
Is that it?

03:44:52 --> 03:44:55
That's only, how long is that?

03:44:54 --> 03:44:57
- Four hours with Marc Andreessen

03:44:56 --> 03:45:01
is like 40 hours of actual content so-

03:45:00 --> 03:45:02
I'll accept being one of the short ones.

03:45:01 --> 03:45:04
(both laughing)

03:45:03 --> 03:45:05
- For the listener.

03:45:04 --> 03:45:08
Marc looks like he's ready to go for 20 more hours,

03:45:08 --> 03:45:11
and I need a nap. (laughs)

03:45:10 --> 03:45:12
Thank you, Marc.

03:45:11 --> 03:45:13
- Thank you, Lex.

03:45:12 --> 03:45:14
- Thanks for listening

03:45:13 --> 03:45:16
to this conversation with Marc Andreessen.

03:45:15 --> 03:45:18
To support this podcast,

03:45:17 --> 03:45:20
please check out our sponsors in the description.

03:45:19 --> 03:45:23
And now, let me leave you with some words

03:45:22 --> 03:45:24
from Thomas Sowell.

03:45:24 --> 03:45:30
"It takes considerable knowledge just to realize the extent

03:45:29 --> 03:45:32
of your own ignorance."

03:45:32 --> 03:45:36
Thank you for listening and hope to see you next time.

<!-- YOUTUBE_TRANSCRIPT_END -->
